--- PAGE 6 ---
CAUTIONS TO TAKE WHEN INSTALLING THE INTERSWITCH, NQE-889
FOR RADAR SERIES, JMA-8000/6000.

GENERAL

BY USING THIS INTERSWIICH, THE RADARS CAN BE SWITCHED-OVER
AT THE DISPLAY UNIT.
THIS SWITCH-OVER WILL DISPLAY THE FOLLOWING SWITCH-OVER

MENU ON THE CRT OF THE DISPLAY UNIT. IT USES THE SELECTOR
METHOD.

INTER-SWITCH
(MODE SELECT>

0: EXIT
: STRAIGHT
2 : CROSS
3:
4:
5:
6:

MODE.-1
MODE-2
MODE-3
MODE-4

SELECT MODE
PRESS ENT

SWITCH-OVER OPERATION

'SHITCH—OVER.CUNDITION IS DISPLAYED WITH SYMBOLS.

FOR EXAMPLE, MODE-I MEANS THAT THE DISPLAY UNIT ON THE
PORT SIDE 1S THE MASTER (POSSIBLE TO CONTROL THE TRANSMITTER
-RECEIVER UNIT AND ANTENNA UNIT) AND THE DISPLAY UNIT ON THE
STARBOARD SIDE IS THE SLAVE UNIT.

--- PAGE 7 ---
3.CAUTIONS TO TAKE AT THE TIME OF INSTALLTION

IN ORDER TO MAKE THE SYMBOL ON THE CRT TO COINCIDE YITH THE ACTUAL
SWITCH-OVER OPERATION, IT IS NECESSARY TO CONNECT THE CABLES FROM THE
DISPLAY UNIT ON THE PORT SIDE TO TB701,TB703,TB705,TB707,TB709,TB711
AND TB713 OF THE INTERSWITCH TERMINAL BOARD.

FURTHER, IT IS NECESSARY TO CONNECT THE CABLES FROM THE DISPLAY
UNIT ON THE STARBOARD SIDE TO TB702,TB704,TB706,TB708,TB710,TB712 AND
TB714.

4. IN ORDER TO PREPARE THE DRAWINGS FOR APPROVAL,PLEASE INSERT YOUR
REQUIREMENTS ON THE FOLLOWING DRAWING AND RETURN TO JRC.

IN THIS SYSTEM THE OVERALL SCHEMATIC DIAGRAM AND INTER-UNIT
CONNECTION DIAGRAM WILL BE CHANGED DEPENDING UPON THE LOCATION OF THE
DISPLAY UNIT.

AS THE LOCATION OF THE DISPLAY UNITS ARE RELATED IN THE PREPARATION
OF THE DRAWINGS FOR APPROVAL, THERFORE, FILL-IN THE ATTACHED FORM AND
RETURN TO JRC.

CAUTIONS TO TAKE WHEN INSTALLING THE INTERSWITCH, NQE-889 , FOR

RADAR SERIES, JMA-8000/6000.

1. GENERAL
BY USING THIS INTERSWITCH, THE RADARS CAN BE SWITCHED-OVER AT
THE DISPLAY UNIT.

THIS SWITCH-OVER WILL DISPLAY THE FOLLOWING SWITCH-OVER MENU ON

THE DISPLAY UNIT. IT USES THE SELECTOR METHOD.,

INTER-SWITCH
<MODE SELECT>

EXIT
STRAIGHT
CROSS
MODE-1
MODE-2
MODE-3
MODE-4

SELECT MODE
PRESS ENT

2. SWITCH-OVER OPERATION

SWITCH-OVER CONDITION IS DISPLAYED WITH SYMBOLS.
FOR EXAMPLE, MODE-1 MEANS THAT THE DISPLAY UNIT ON THE PORT
SIDE IS THE MASTER (POSSIBLE TO CONTROL THE TRANSMITTER-RECEIVER

UNIT AND ANTENNA UNIT) AND THE DISPLAY UNIT ON THE STARBOARD SIDE

IS THE SLAVE UNIT.

)

--- PAGE 38 ---
3.CAUTIONS TO TAKE AT THE TIME OF INSTALLTION

IN ORDER TO MAKE THE SYMBOL ON THE CRT TO COINCIDE WITH THE ACTUAL
SWITCH-OVER OPERATION, IT IS NECESSARY TO CONNECT THE CABLES FROM THE
DISPLAY UNIT ON THE PORT SIDE TO TB701,TB703,TB705,TB707,TB708,TB711
AND TB713 OF THE INTERSWITCH TERMINAL BOARD.

FURTHER, IT IS NECESSARY TO CONNECT THE CABLES FROM THE DISPLAY
UNIT ON THE STARBOARD SIDE TO TB702,TB704,TB706,TB708,TB710,TB712 AND
TB714.

1. IN ORDER TO PREPARE THE DRAWINGS FOR APPROVAL,PLEASE INSERT YOUR
REQUIREMENTS ON THE FOLLOWING DRAWING AND RETURN TO JRC.

IN THIS SYSTEM THE OVERALL SCHEMATIC DIAGRAM AND INTER-UNIT
CONNECTION DIAGRAM WILL BE CHANGED DEPENDING UPON THE LOCATION OF THE
DISPLAY UNIT.

AS THE LOCATION OF THE DISPLAY UNITS ARE RELATED IN THE PREPARATION
OF THE DRAWINGS FOR APPROVAL, THERFORE, FILL-IN THE ATTACHED FORM AND
RETERN TO JRC. ] :

PRECAUTIONS BEFORE OPERATION

CAUTIONS FOR HIGH VOLTAGE

High voltages of hundreds upto thousands volt are used in radio and radar devices. Although
prudent measures for safety have been adopted, sufficient cares should be taken on operation,,
maintenance and adjustment of the device.

Electric shock by thousands volt leads to an instantaneous death for certain, and even electric
shock by hundreds volt leads to an instantaneous death occasionally. To prevent such an accident,
turn off the power source, discharge capacitors by a wire surely earthed at one end, and check
that any charge is no longer inside the device, before you put your hand into the inside. It is still
better to wear dry wool gloves. It is also necessary caution not to use both hands simultaneously,
by putting the one hand into your pocket.

The selection of a stable foothold is essential to prevent more extensive or additional injuries.
When injured by electric shock, disinfect the burn perfectly and give first aid as soon as possible.

HEALTH HAZARD

Some person allergic to dust may temporarily be affected in health, if he inspires dust scattered
at the time of cleaning the radar inside.

RADIATION HAZARD

If the human body is exposed to a microwave of 10mW/cm? in mean power density, it will be
affected, in particular, at the eyes.

Do not approach to any antenna during standstill as near as 60cm or less.

If it is unavoidable by any means to disconnect a microwave transmission line upon the
maintenance or checks, always connect a microwave dummy load to associated radar transmitter.
If it is unavoidable to radiate the microwave without using any microwave dummy load, never
peep the active waveguide into its end during transmission, since the end is open.

WHAT TO DO IN CASE OF ELECTRIC SHOCK

When a victim of electric shock is found, turn off the power source and earth the circuit
immediately. If this is impossible, move the victim away from it without touching him with bare
hands as quick as possible. He can safely be moved if an insulating material such as dry wood
plate or cloth is used.

Breathing may stop if current flows through the respiration center of brain due to electric shock.
If the electric shock is not large, breathing can be restored by artificial respiration. A victim of
electric shock looks pale and his pulse becomes very weak or stops, resulting in unconsciousness
and rigidity.

--- PAGE 74 ---
FIRST-AID TREATMENTS

As far as the victim of electric shock is not in dangerous condition, artificial respiration should
be started at once at the site. Once started it should be continued rhythmically.

1) Do not touch the victim confusedly or rescuer may also receive an electric shock.
2) Tumn off the power source and move the victim from the electric line calmly.

3) Call a physician or ambulance immediately, or instruct someone else to call.

4) Place the victim on his back and loose his necktie, clothes, belt, etc.

5) Examine the victim’s pulse.

Examine the heartbeat by laying your ear on his heart.
Examine breathing by bringing your face or back of hand close to his face.
Examine the victim’s pupils.

o o

6) Open the victim’s mouth and remove the artificial teeth, cigarette or chewing gum, if any.
Keeping the mouth open, stretch the tongue and insert a towel or the like to prevent the
tongue from suffocating. (If it is hard to open the mouth with set teeth, open it by a
screwdriver and insert a towel.)

7) Wipe the mouth to prevent foamable mucus and saliva from accumulating.

IN CASE PULSE CAN BE DETECTED BUT BREATHING STOPS

(Mouth to mouth artificial respiration)

a) Tilt the victim’s heat back as far as his face looks back.
(A pillow may be inserted under the neck.)

b) Pull the jaw into jutting position to open the throat.

¢) Pinch the victim’s nostrils shut and place your mouth tightly over his after drawing your
breath long. Then blow into mouth strongly. Continue blowing at a rate of 10-15 breathes
a minute.

d) Watch carefully and continue artificial respiration till natural respiration is restored.

3) If the opening of mouth is difficult, insert a vinyl pipe or the like into a nostril and blow
into it by shutting the other nostril and mouth perfectly.

f)  Victim may stand up suddenly when he becomes aware. He should lie quietly and kept
warm and calm.
Serve him with hot coffee or tea (but never alcoholic drinks).

Method of Mouth to Mouth artificial respiration

Tilting Back of Victim’s Head

Put one of your hands on the victim’s forehead and the other under the neck @®. In general his
mouth opens when the head is tilted back, making easy mouth to mouth artificial respiration.
Place your mouth tightly over his and press your check against his nose (D or pinch his nostrils
by your fingers (® to prevent air leakage.

--- PAGE 75 ---
Blowing into Lungs
Blow into the mouth till the chest rises. The first 10 breathes should be performed as fast as
possible.

IN CASE OF CARDIAC ARREST AND CESSATION OF BREATHING

(Cardiac massage and mouth to mouth artificial respiration)

a)

b)

When no pulse can be detected, the pupils are open and no heartbeat is heard, cardiac arrest
is considerable.

Therefore, artificial respiration should be started at once.

Put the heel of one hand over the lower 1/3 of his breastbone and the other hand on the
back of the first. Apply your weight so that the breastbone is compressed by about 2 cm.
(Repeat it at a rate of about 50 times a minute).

(Cardiac massage)

c)

d)

In case of one rescuer
After about 15 times cardiac massages, give mouth artificial respiration 2 times, and repeat
them in this manner.

In case of two rescuers
While the victim is massaged, another person should give mouth to mouth artificial respiration.
A breath is inter posed after 5 cardiac massages.

--- PAGE 76 ---
(Cardiac massage and mouth artificial respiration)

e) Examine the pupils and pulse often. When they become normal, stop the first aid treatments,
serve the victim with coffee or tea and keep him warm and calm, while watching him
carefully.

f)  Commit the victim to a medical specialist, according to the situation. For earlier recovery
of the victim from the mental shock, the understanding of bystanders is essential.

SCANNER RADIATION HAZARD

Injury may result from exposure to the main beam of a stationary Radar Scanner at a distance
from the central front face of the aerial less than:

meter for 7 foot Scanner (Radiation power density of 100W/m?)
meter for 7 foot Scanner (Radiation power density of 10W/m?)
meter for 9 foot Scanner (Radiation power density of 100W/m?)
meter for 9 foot Scanner (Radiation power density of 10W/m?)

Care should be taken to avoid possible harmful effect (particularly to the eyes) of radiation from
radar transmission.

To avoid harmful radiation, the POWER switch should be turned to the ST-BY or OFF position
or SCANNER switch to OFF position when working on the Antenna.

NEVER LOOK DOWN A RADIATOR WHICH POWER IS BEING RADIATED FROM A
DISTANCE OF LESS THAN ABOVE!

L2 COLLISION AVOIDANCE

.-..-... Collision Avoidance Problems in Navigation ........ccccccemmmmeneeeee..

The ship’s collision avoidance is one of the navigational problems, which has been recognized
since early times. This section describes briefly the position of the collision avoidance as a
navigational problems.

The navigation pattern of every vehicle seems to configure a system having several closed loops,
irrespective of the type of media, through which the vehicle passes, such as air, water, cosmic
space, etc., including their boundaries. This pattern may consist of two closed loops, in general.
One is for avoidance of collision against other vehicles. The other is for finding a safety and
correct route to a determined destination.

E.W. Anderson gave an outline of the navigation pattern, which is shown in Figure 1.1. The
closed loop for collision avoidance is given on the left of this diagram and the other closed loop
for finding the course on the right.

MENT

v Disaster at Sea and ColliSIiON.......o.ooveevieiercerierre e snsronsmnsressnsens

The collision trouble among disasters at sea has been highlighted as the tonnage and speed of
ships increase as well as the traffic quantity at sea increases.

If a tanker carrying a dangerous products such as crude oil, or similar vessel collides against
other ship, then not only the vessels concerned will run into trouble, but other vessels around
them and port facilities near them will also suffer damage from the collision trouble, as well as
inhabitants along nearby coast, fishery resources, etc. Thus, the associated influences will be
inestimably profound.

In addition, the proportion of the collision trouble becomes considerably large among the disasters
at sea in recent years.

To cope with such situation, some countermeasures are necessary for preventing vessels from
collision. Development of new equipment has been, therefore, hastened to meet such requirements.

--- PAGE 81 ---
1.2 Collision Avoidance ®
HNENNEF NN NN NN SN SN EEEEEE NN SN NN EEEEE NN RN NN NN EEEEEENNENENEENEENEEEEEEES

........ Basic Concept of Collision Avoidance..........cccceerrrerrrrereeseemerserenns

The collision avoidance has two aspects: Prediction and avoidance of collision. Extremely
speaking, the prediction of collision is to predict that a plurality of vessels will occupy the same
point at once. The avoidance of collision is to steer the vessel not so as to occupy the same
point.
It is necessary for steering the vessel in actual practice to consider not a pinpoint but a closed
area. This area has been conceived as CPA (closest point of approach). In addition, the prediction
of collision requires a time taken for arrival at the CPA. This time has been conceived as TCPA
(time to CPA). It serves for determination of the danger of collision.
FIG. 1.2 illustrates a chart called “collision triangle”.

CPA TCPA (time to CPA)

e RElAtiVve Vector and True VeCtor ... e ceeeeeesrcsseseseeseesessenssmnnns

Seeing from two points of view, prediction and avoidance of collision described above, both a
relative-speed vector and true-speed vector are required for prediction and avoidance of collision,
respectively. The latter vector is necessary to know the aspect of other ships.

Figure 1.3 illustrates the relationship between the relative-speed and true-speed vectors.

Now, the meanings of both types of vectors are described below.

The relative-speed vector first suggests approximate CPA and TCP easy to fine. This will give
a profit for the user to take hold of the degrees of collision between all the vessels appearing on
the radar scope, at a glance.

The true-speed vector, on the other hand, will inform the speed and course of other vessel also
easy to take hold. This advantages the user to find the aspect at a glance. Owing to this type of
vector, the user can take hold of the traffic situation described in the Prevention of Collision at
Sea Act (such as transverse, outsailing, parallel running, reverse running, etc.).

Where other vessels located in dangerous areas, the user can find what rule applies and how to
steer own ship.

1.2 Collision Avoidance : ®

e RAdar and ColliSion AVOIdANCE .....eeeeeeeeeceecercrvecemeeernar s ensemssnasmsnns

The radar is still now playing an important role for prevention from collision, as well as available
for measuring the position.

The radar employs a plotter to further improve its ability. The plotter displays plots of the
locations of other vessels at intervals of 3 minutes or 6 minutes to know their movements. These
plots describe traces of the other vessels’ run, relative to own ship. From the traces, the user can
easily find the possibility of the collision, CPA and TCPA.

This method is said considerably effective, however, requires labour for plotting. This will limit
the number of target vessels under plotting and requires several minutes for measurement.

........ Basic Concept of System ... ——————————————-

[

[2]

[3]

14]

The system with use of a radar as a sensor has several functional stages described below.

First stage : Target detection from radar information

This corresponds to a function for manually plotting targets on the plotter of the radar. Now,
assume one vessel appearing near own ship. It can be caught by the radar. The signal of this
target is processed in a data processor and transferred to a computer as a bearing and range
signals relative to own ship. This stage contains functions for quantizing the radar information,
rejection of noise and eliminating other than vessels information, in addition to the functions for
transferring the position data of the vessels to the computer.

Second stage: Target tracking

“Tracking” defined in this system corresponds to a function for electrically plotting data at fixed
time intervals. On the other, the plotter of the radar displays plots at intervals of three minutes
or six minutes.

In this stage, the position data of targets detected in time are compared with previously detected
position data to check whether the targets are identical or not. Data is filed for each identical
target to calculate the change the position data of the identical target.

Third stage: Determination of vessel in danger of collision

This stage is to determine the possibility of collision after calculation of the speed and course of
a target under track, from its position data varying in time in the second stage. If the speed and
course of the target are found, then the closest point of approach to own ship (CPA=closest point
of approach) and the time required for arrival at the CPA (TCPA = time to CPA) can easily be
calculated.

Both CPA and TCPA are compared with presetted MIN. CPA and MIN. TCPA according to the
condition of own ship to determine whether it is in danger of collision or not.

Fourth stage; Indication

Above information must be informed to the officers steering the ship, with certain indication.
This system is provided with features for displaying a video and speed vectors of other vessels
(with alternate switch of the true- and relative-speed vectors) with indication of two types of
marks on targets on the usual radar scope. One type is for indicating a danger vessel, and the
other type for indicating a safe vessel. If a vessel is in danger of collision, the alarm sound is
produced and alarm lamps illuminate to alert the officers.

[TM/RM] TRUE AND RELATIVE MOTION SELECT SWITCH
Change the display to true-motion or to relative-motion.

[RESET] RESET SWITCH
Set own ship’s position back to starting point in True-motion mode.

[SHM OFF] SHIP HEAD MARKER ERASING SWITCH
While keep on pressing this switch, SHM disappears.

{[RANGE RINGS] FIXED RANGE MARKER DISPLAY SWITCH
On and off the fixed range marker.

[RESET/VOL] ALARM RESET AND ALARM SOUND CONTROL -
To stop the alarm sound or to control the alarm sound.

[TRAIL] TRAIL DISPLAY SWITCH
To present other ship’s trail.

[PROCESS] PROCESSED PICTURE DISPLAY SWITCH
Fade out unwanted picture and intensify useful picture in 3 degrees.

[X 2] EXPANDING SWITCH
To enlarge target or area around own ship.

[CONTRAST] CONTRAST CONTROL
Control contrast of picture on the display.

--- PAGE 90 ---
®

[RANGE] RANGE SELECT SWITCH
To select range from 0.25 to 120 miles.

[OFF CENTER] OFF CENTER SWITCH
To shift own ship on display so that to expand the area of optional direction.

[BEARING] BEARING SELECT SWITCH
To select display mode among North-up, Head-up and Course-up.

[DATA ENTRY] DATA INPUT TEN-KEY
To enter data in digits.

[TRACK] OWN SHIP’S TRAIL DISPLAY SWITCH

In case combined with Navigational equipments, displays own ship’s trail by receiving
data from that.

[GUARD RING] GUARD RING SETTING SWITCH
To set up Guard ring to get alarm whenever other ships or objects enter the ring.

[CENTER/INDP] EBL CENTER/INDEPENDENT MODE SWITCH
Select EBL in center or in independent mode.

[CURSOR] PARALLEL LINE CURSOR SWITCH
Displays Parallel Lines in half or full circle of the screen.

[PLOT] PLOTTING SWITCH
To locate Plot mark on optional position on the display.

[CLEAR] PLOTTING CLEAR SWITCH
To clear off the plot mark.

[VRM1/VRM?2] VARJIABLE RANGE MARKER SWITCH
To display and setting up variable range markets VRM1 and VRM2.

[ENTER] ENTER SWITCH
To ENTER CURSOR-selected function.

TRACK BALL
To move Cursor mark on the display.

[TRUE/REL] TRUE AND RELATIVE VECTOR SELECT SWITCH
To select true or relative vector of the target under tracking.

[LENGTH] VECTOR LENGTH SETTING SWITCH

[PAST POS] PAST POSITION ON/OFF AND TIME INTERVAL SETTING SWITCH

[A LINE] AUTO SUPPRESSION LINE “A” SETTING SWITCH
To set up suppression line to eliminate unwanted acquisition area in Auto Acquisition
Mode.

[B LINE] AUTO SUPPRESSION LINE “B” SETTING SWITCH
To set up suppression line to eliminate unwanted acquisition area in Auto Acquisition
Mode.

[SHIP/LAND] AUTO SUPPRESSION LINE TO FIX ON LAND/SHIP SELECT SWITCH

[RQST DATA] DIGITAL TARGET DATA REQUEST SWITCH

[AUTO DRIFT] AUTO DRIFT APPOINT SWITCH

[RING] SHORT RANGE AUTO SUPERSESSION RING SET SWITCH
To set up the area to eliminate auto acquisition of unwanted targets.

[MANUAL] MANUAL ACQUISITION SWITCH
To acquire target manually.

[AUTO] AUTO ACQUISITION SWITCH
To acquire target automatically.

[CANCEL] TRACKING CANCEL SWITCH
To clear off the symbol and vector of the target under tracking and cancel the target
tracking.

'=3.€— PREPARATION

S K o TR 1 { o =

TUNE

A

The knob is to tune the receiver.

Tuning adjustment if deviated from the optimum tuning point, degrade the receiver sensitivity
and lost the small targets in the vicinity of Own ship or overlook the objects in the long distance.
Tuning adjustment to be done by turning [TUNE] knob left or right to get the clearest picture of
the targets. ,

When there are no proper targets available, turn the Tuning knob to make the Tuning Bar Graph
longer to the right direction.

As the frequency of magnetron become steady after about 10 minutes, tuning should to be carried
out again in more than 10 minutes after transmission started.

SR O« 1 31 { o 1 BN €7 ¥ J 1 |

GAIN

This knob is to control receiver gain. Turning [GAIN] knob clockwise makes receiver gain to
increase enabling to expand the distance and area of the good Radar picture observation.

If the gain is increased in excess, the noises are also increased to deteriorate the contrast of the
targets and the receiving noise. In case where the targets are congested or the area in more
shorter range is required to be observed, turn the knob in anti-clockwise to decrease the gain to
make screen more easy to see. However, care must be taken not to overlook the small and
important targets.

CRT BRIL

The knob is to adjust brightness of whole screen. Tuming to clockwise makes the screen brighter.
Adjust as observer feels best to see easily.

ceeneens SUPPrESS Sea-ClUtter ...ttt

SEA

=<

AUTO

The knob is to decrease the receiving sensitivity in the short range so as to decrease the Sea-
clutter noise. Turning the knob in clockwise increase the effect of Anti-Sea Clutter. However,
care must be taken that the excess effect shall not fade out the bouys or small ships in a short
range.

Turn the knob to the AUTO position makes the control in optimum condition automatically.

cenenee SUPPress Rain/SNowW-ClUtter ... sssassasssenes

RAIN

The knob is to suppress Rain or Show clutter noises. Turning the knob in clockwise makes the
hidden targets outline to appear out of noise back ground. However, this control often makes
small targets to be overlooked.

Combined use of [ANTI CLUTTER SEA] knob will makes the performance more effective.
Normally, this knob is to be turned in fully counter- clockwise.

--- PAGE 99 ---
Reject Radar Interference [IR] ........cccmmmmminiciimmimm e

IR

The Radar interference caused by same frequency band can be rejected by this switch. One press makes
function “ON” and further each press of 3 steps increase the effect of rejection gradually. Last one more
press makes function back to “OFF” condition. The effects are also to decrease Sea-clutters and Receive
noises. Combined use of this function helps to stabilize the tracking process while tracking the targets in
ARPA mode.

Care must be taken that by using Radar Interference rejection facility, weak signals are tends to be faded
out.

the operation of the Track Ball up and down moves the Cursor Mark [+] up and down. At the

PULSE
WIDTH

K

This switch changes [LONG [1] transmitting pulse in 3 kinds : Long [LONG 1], Medium
[MEDIUM JL ] and Short [SHORT JL] by pressing one by one and selected pulse width is
indicated upper left corner of the Display. The selection is only effective for the range scale of
3,6 and 12 miles. When [SHORT J1] is selected, distance resolution is to be improved. When
[LONG 1] is selected, distance resolution is to be degraded but sensitivity is improved and
small target can be seen larger and easy to observe.

~.. Change Bearing Indication.........cccccoemreirrsrsscssenscsceecerenr e

This switch changes the presentation of bearing in <HEAD UP> and <COURSE UP> respectively
by each pressing.

BEARING

|

“NORTH UP” presentation:

In this mode, True Gyro North is always coincided with “O” degree point of the display’s
fixed bearing scale. This is very easy to refer to the chart and advantageous to read the true
bearing of the targets immediately.

“HEAD UP” presentation:

In this mode, Ship’s Head Marker always is coincided with “O” degree point of the display’s
fixed bearing scale. Targets are displayed relative to ship’s head so the picture on the
display is as same as the eyesight from the navigation bridge when the ship is in maneuver.

“COURSE UP” presentation:

In this mode, SHM is displayed coincide with the “O” degree point of the fixed bearing
scale simultaneously with the switch is pressed. Similar to the NORTH UP presentation,
targets are displayed steadily on the screen regardless to the ship’s yawing and only the
bearing of SHM fnoves as much as the ship’s course varied. When the ship took a large
course change, press the switch 3 times so as to reset the COURSE-UP presentation.

SHM OFF

|

Ship’s Heading Mark (SHM) indicates own ship’s proceeding course and is constantly displayed
on the screen. To obtain clear view in ship’s head direction, this SHM can be erased for the
duration of <SHM OFF> switch (spring return) being pressed.

FULL DISPLAY Semi-circle Display

........ Changing True/Relative Motion Presentation ............ccccccrnracnmarnnn

Relative Motion is the normal presentation when the Radar is in stated operation.
The presentation can be changed to True Motion Mode by following Procedures.

Froceiues B

I -

TM/RM RESET

Press [TM/RM] switch.

Lamp in switch center illuminates and picture changes to True Motion Mode. In True-
Motion mode, stationary target such as Land and bouy are displayed as stationary and actually
moving own-ship and other targets are displayed as per its own speed and course as a moving
targets. When switched to True Motion Mode, own ship’s position shifts to the point about

65% behind forward direction of the Radius of the screen along the Ship’s Heading Marker.

The own ship starts to proceed along the course and when reached to the point about 65%
forward radius direction, return to the original starting point automatically.

Should the situation require to bring the ship to original stating point without wait for automatic
return, see procedure 2.

Press [RESET] switch.

Wherever the ship’s position is, ship can be brought back to the original starting point and
starts moving up again.

Press Once more [TM/RM] switch.

Lamp goes off and picture returns to Relative Motion Mode bringing own ship’s position in
center of the screen automatically.

—4_ 2 MEASUREMENT OF CPA & TCPA

These procedures are to determine Relative maneuver of another ship approaching to own ship.
To measure CPA (Closest point of Approach) and TCPA (Time to CPA) is the most fundamental
and routine works to be Performed on board. In this system by using EBL, informations related
with Collision Avoidance can easily be obtained. :

Procirs B

By using electronic plotter, acquire a position (A) of the target ship. At the
same time, registry of plotting time is made.

. After some minutes (e.x. 3 minutes), take a plotting (B) of the same target ship.

. Put the Cursor Mark on first target ship (A) and press [CENTER/INDP] switch

twice.

. Turn [EBL] bearing adjust knob and put EBL on target ship plotted by above

procedure 2.

. Adjust VRM until it contacts with EBL.

Contact point of VRM and EBL to be called “P” then the distance between Ownship and
point P is the CPA.

. Distance between point A and B and distance between point B and Ownship

can be measured by variable Range Marker.

TCPA can be obtained by following formula.
TCPA = &  t (min)

For example: A target ship came to point B 3 minutes after from A. Distance between A
and B is 0.5NM and between B and P is SNM.
The formula comes to:

TCPA = 0—55 x 3 = 30 ( (min)

Relative speed of the target ship against Own - ship can be obtained by follow formula. (By
using Speed Table 4.1, answer available quickly).

# MEASUREMENT OF APPROACHING SHIP'S COURSE AND SPEED

Following are the procedures to obtain TRUE COURSE and TRUE SPEED of the other ship
approaching to own-ship.

Froeir: B

By using Electronic plotter, acquire target - ship’s position (A). At the same
time plotting time to be recorded.

. After few minutes (e.x. 3 minutes), plot the same target - ship’s position (B).

. Put the starting point on position (A) by Track Ball and Press [CENTER/INDP]

switch twice.

. By turning [EBL] bearing adjust knob, make the EBL in parallel with the SHM

line yet in opposite direction.

. Move the VRM marker on the EBL from starting point to a point (C) of the

Distance made by own-ship’s elapsed time and put plot mark on it. (Assuming
that own-ship’s speed is 12 knots and elapsed time is 3 minutes, the distance
required now is 0.6NM).

. Put EBL starting point on position “C” and true [EBL] bearing adjust knob so

that EBL aligned with plot mark “B”.

The bearing obtained by this operation is the target-ship’s true course.

. True speed can be obtained from the distance and observed time between

points (B) and (C).

For example, if the VRM reading were INM and observed time were 3 minutes, True speed
comes to 20 KTS by following formula
INM x 60 MIN

TS = — S MIN = 20 KTS same can be obtained easily from Table 4.1.

—A SETTING UP COLLISION AVOIDANCE COURSE AND SPEED

Procedures

In Radar observation, if a target is constantly in own-ship’s fairway, there is a risk of collision.
Should the reading of CPA is dangerous, course or speed must be changed. In FIG. 4.6, reading
of CPA is “0”NM and is sure to collide.

In order to avoid collision, procedures are made to change own-ship’s course so that CPA against
the target is to be set up in 2 Nautical miles. Therefore, CPA changes from ONM to 2NM.

1.

Plot the Targetship’s position (A) by Electronic plotter and at the same time,
record the time of plotting.

. After few minutes (e.x. 3 min), Plot the position (B) of the same Targetship.
. Display VRM of radius 2NM around own-ship in center.

. Draw a tangential line from above point (B) to the above VRM of radius 2NM

and point of contact to be defined as (P).

. Put EBL starting point on (P) and adjust [EBL] bearing adjust knob to contact

EBL on point (B). On this extended line, plot the point (Q).

. Replace the line between (P) and (Q) with a NAV line.
. Put starting point on point (A) and press [CENTER/INDP] switch twice.

. Adjust [EBL] bearing adjust knob so that EBL runs in parallel with ship head-

ing line yet in opposite direction.

. Move the VRM Marker on the EBL from starting point to (C) of the distance

made by own - ship’s elapsed time and put plot mark on it.

10.Put EBL starting point on point (C) and turn [EBL] bearing adjust knob so that

to obtain point (D) the crossing point of VRM marker and the line between (P)
& (Q).

The obtained Bearing line C-D is the New collision avoidance course to be taken by own-
ship. Otherwise by decreasing speed of own-ship from A-C speed to A-E speed, similar
relative maneuvering action to be taken is available.

—._ 5 CALIBRATION OF COURSE AND SPEED

When the ship is maneuvering in the adverse weather such as fog, tide or in the strong wind, it
is necessary to calibrate the speed and course correctly specially along the unfamiliar coasts or
waters.

Following procedure shows one example that own ship is in a position from where the pen-
insula located in Bearing 29° and distance 4.5NM at 14:30 Hours.

1. Draw a NAV LINE from the tip of Peninsula in parallel with ship Heading Marker.

2. After 15 minutes, measures the bearing distance of the same tip of the Penin-
sula.

Assuming that the bearing 53° and distance 2NM were measured at 14:45 Hours, this is
obviously the own-ship is drifted inside theoretical relative motion line.

3. Put EBL starting point on tip of the peninsula registered at 14:45 Hours.

4. Turn [EBL] bearing adjust knob and contact EBL on tip of the peninsula regis-
tered at 14:30 Hours.

The bearing indicated by the EBL shows the deviation of the course in 11° starboard.

6th ring become blank as in Fig. 4.9, Radar’s overall performance degraded for about -5db.
If both 6th and 5th rings are totally blank, overall performance is lowered about -10db.

4.6 APPLICATION OF VARIOUS OPTIONS eee

By counting the number of blanked arc-shaped ring, Radar’s overall performance can be analyzed.
However for further precise analysis to discern whether cause of the trouble is in transmitting
circuit that the transmitting power is decreased or is in receiving circuit that from the Duplexer
mixer up to the receiver unit the performance being dropped. The method of analysis are as
follows:

1. Measure the angle of Arc-shaped ring at the time of initial setting (FIG. 4.8
angle 6,) and keep the record.

2. Measure the angle of Arc-shaped ring at the time of overall performance dropped
by -5db (FIG. 4.9 Angle 6,).

It is presumable that when 6=6,, the receiver circuit is in trouble and when 6, < 6,, the
transmitter circuit is in failure.

3. Fillup the PERFORMANCE MONITOR INFORMATION lavel (See FIG. 4.12) in

accordance with the example of description that date, tuning position, Arc ring
starting angle (START) and ending angle (END) are to be recorded.

1. Press [BEARING] switch and set Radar on [HEAD UP] Mode.

2. Reset off-center presentation.
3. Press [CENTER/INDP] switch.

Following additional functions are available by connecting Total Navigation System (SNA-90).
Digital indication of own ship’s position in Lat/Long.

Digital indication of Lat/Long, of an object picked up by Track Ball Cursor.

Display of electronic chart sent out from SNA-90.

Correction of Electronic chart made of Radar Video.

Presentation and correction of course informations sent out from SNA-90.

Establishment and correction of course on Radar screen.

Presentation of simulation trail over-lapping on displayed course.

Memorization of displayed course (in SNA-90).

Transfer of ARPA tracking targets into SNA-90.

Operations of Total Navigation system are as follows:

[f] Total navigation system ON/OFF.

1. Press [ON/RECALL] switch.

[XI]] Geographical limit of presentation.

Should any point of higher Latitude than 85° North or South exist in the Electronic chart, the
warning message <OVER 85 DEGREE> is displayed and Electnic chart is not presented.
Electronic chart wider than 60 NM from own-ship can not be presented. In this case,
warning message is <OUT OF SCREEN> displayed on the screen.

Planned course further than 60 NM cannot be established. Line of limitation will be shown
to prevent further procedure.

Electronic chart may not be displayed correctly in the area around or more than 79° Latitude.
When SNA-90 is not ready for the data reception, warning message <NOT READY> is
displayed.

5.3 TARGET AcCQuISITION

The system has the 2 methods of target acquisition, Automatic and Manual, or both can be used
at the same time.

ceeeeeee AUtOMatic AcquiSItion [AUTO].....ccconireeermsesrsncsssnessassmsnssssssassesssnses

AUTO

1. Press [AUTO] switch on ARPA operation panel.

Lamp illuminates and Auto-acquisition starts. Acquired targets are labelled with <[ AJ> Mark
which moves together with the target and the Vector appears within one minute.

Standard of Target Selection

Order of Priority of Automatic acquisition area:
First Priority area I: 315° - 45° forward sector
2nd Priority area II: 45° - 90° starboard sector
3rd Priority area III: 270° - 315° port sector
4th Priority area IV: 90° - 270° aft sector

Scanner rotating direction

SHM

Approx 315° Approx 45°

Approx 270° Approx 90°

End mark (a picture block)

Acquisition priority area

--- PAGE 165 ---
Accordingly, immediately after above ACQUIRE [AUTO] switch is pressed, targets in order of
scanner rotating direction are acquired and tracked (in First priority area, acquisition is made in
every scan). The system effectively catches the targets in forward area in priority. Further
explanation will be made later however, at the time of using Auto-acquisition mode, not only
combined use of Manual acquisition’s availability but also means to establish optional acquisition
excluding area is possible that the system can be effectively used even in the area where there
are congested land mass and heavy sea clutters are existed.

2. These priority area are for the preliminary procession of the automatic acquisition and
is somehow rather rough that in the area where there are too many targets, some of the
targets may be acquired from the lower priority area.

........ Combined Use of Auto/Manual Acquisition [AUTO/MANUAL] ...

ACQUIRE
MANUAL AUTO CANCEL

o] Lo [e]

Operation
Carry out Manual acquisition while the system is in Auto-acquisition Mode.
Usage of Combination Mode

The purpose of combined usage is to manually acquire the specially remarked target and other
targets are left acquired automatically. By doing so, whenever new targets are entered over the
maximum targets number, this manually acquired targets remain on the screen and furthest auto-
matically acquired targets goes off one by one.

When the number of targets are few and within the maximum number, Automatic acquisition
only will be sufficient. However, when the targets are so many and in autoacquisition mode,
acquired targets are all concentrated in forward first priority zone only. In such case, use of
manual acquisition is indispensable.

The combination mode is to acquire remarked targets first and let rest of all targets in
autoacquisition so as to maintain choice of manual selection during acquiring new targets auto-
matically.

.--..... Setting Up Acquire Suppression Area: SUPPRESSION AREA......

Setting up of acquire suppression area can be made independently from acquisition mode (AUTO
& MANUAL ). However, manual acquisition is not to be restricted by this suppression area.
There are 2 kinds of suppressors that one is SUPPRESSION LINE, outside this line, any targets
or lands are eliminated from autoacquisition and another is SUPPRESSION RING, inside of this
ring, own ship’s vicinity can be set up as an area of elimination from autoacquisition.

This function is used in such case when the sea clutter is too heavy and unable to control by any
means. The purpose is to prevent occurrence of wrong informations in such heavy noise as sea
clutter by eliminating area of auto-acquisition in the vicinity of own-ship. Accordingly, it is
important not to make this suppression area too large to neglect important targets in this area.
Auto-acquisition is invalid in this area. However, tracking of the targets acquired outside the
area are continuously tracked even after the entry in this suppression area.

-....... Limiting Factor of Target Acquisition and Tracking ...................

[

]

[

Resolution and swapping of targets in Auto-Tracking.

Target’s resolution in auto-tracking depends on distance and size of echoes but normally about
0.03 to 0.0SNM. However, when the plural number of targets are in a close distance each other,
the system took them as a single target even distance are approx. 0.03NM and swapping or lost
of either target may occur. Likewise, there are the possibility of lost target or swapping of
targets when the targets in tracking enter into rain clutter, or sea clutter, or come very close to
the land objects.

Strength of Echo and Tracking Function

Tracking function depends on the strength of the echoes. If the echoes are not detected continously
in over 6 scans, target may be lost. For that reason, Radar’s gain must be adequate to help take
in the targets. On the contrary, too much Radar gain may pick up and track unwanted noises or
sea clutters and generates false alarm. In such case, it is necessary to decrease gain and elimi-
nate noises.

Affects given to Auto-tracking from Various Sources of Error

In order to obtain clear picture of the targets, combined adjustment of Radar’s [GAIN], [ANTI-
CLUTTER SEA], [ANTI-CLUTTER RAIN] and [IR] are quite important. Without correct ad-
justments, reliability of tracking accuracy will be deteriorated.

(1) Sea clutters and Rain/Snow clutters

Not only correct adjustment of [ANTI-CLUTTER SEA] or [ANTI-CLUTTER RAIN], par-
allel usage of SUPPRESSION RING is recommended specially when the sea clutters are
difficult to eliminate.

(2) Radar Interference

Radar interference can almost be rejected by switching ON [IR] switch. This function also
has effect of decreasing sea clutters and receiver noise that continued use of this function
with ARPA is recommendable.

Accordingly, the system must be thoroughly examined and designed for the purpose to
minimize the errors created by ARPA facility itself of the deterioration of tracking accuracy
to various causes of external errors.

(2) Relative vector presentation

As the started up vector mode is TRUE vector mode, to change the mode to RELATIVE,
press [TRUE/REL] switch and let the switch lamp go off. Relative vector shows relative
relation of the targets against own-ship that any Relative vector pointing toward own-ship
(predictable to pass in MIN CPA ring) is the dangerous target.

In Relative vector mode, the CPA of a target can be seen instantly at a sight.

SHM

Relative vector

True movement, to set up True vector mode and for the information for Closest Point of
Approach, to select Relative Vector mode.

NOTE
In relative vector mode, MIN CPA ring is displayed in the size of predetermined SAFE
LIMIT.

... DiSplay Past Position [PAST POS] .....cccceemiieceeuremsmseeemmcmenemmcenceeees

PAST POS

Whenever required to display target’s trail, press [PAST POS] switch and lamp illuminates.
Maximum 6 points out trails can be displayed. Time interval of the trail for 0.5, 1, 2, and 4 MIN
can be selected by pressing [PAST POS] switch one by one. Further press beyond 4 MIN makes
trail indication in “OFF” position.

Trail mode is synchronized with vector mode and True or Relative trail can be presented. In
relative vector mode, targets bearing and distance are registered and displayed as it is. In True
vector mode true trails, calculated from own-ship’s speed and course with relative bearing and
distance, are displayed.

Display method of trail and past vector for a specific target is as follows:
1. Press APPOINT [RQST DATA] switch.
Lamp flashes.

2. Put cursor mark on the target required to draw a trail and press “twice” the
[ENTER] switch.

A past trail of the designated target with 1 MIN interval of maximum 6 dotted line vector is
displayed.

--- PAGE 177 ---
3. To return back display to all targets trail presentation, press [ENTER] switch
once more.

More older vector are displayed in more shorter dotted line.
Usage of this presentation is convenient in confirming whether target changed course or speed
otherwise to check reliability of the vector of the targets.

data due to the drift by current. In such case, by acquiring such fixed target as a bouy or
anchoring ship and designate them as a drift target, so that True motion and Nav-line display can
be presented correctly.

not taken, position of True motion mode and Nav line may cause large error and eventually,
correction should be made on Nav-Line position or to re-set the True motion mode.

GUARD
RING

o |

The function of Guard Ring is to make an alarm sound whenever any targets from outside of the
ring enter inside of the ring established in an optional range.

The system can establish 2 kinds of Guard Rings that one is fixed and another is variable size of
the ring.

When fixed Guard Ring is not required, set the fixed Guard Ring in “0” -NM and only variable
Guard Ring make available.

When Guard Ring used in combination with manual acquisition mode, alarm will sound when-
ever target enters inside of the ring of predetermined range. If necessary, these targets have to
be acquired manually for tracking. Setting up method for Guard Ring must be referred to sec-
tion 3.3 “Setting up Guard Ring”.

........ Navigation Line Alarm: NAV LINE.........cccccmiiimimmmmminimsmeemmmccccccenenes

When own ship’s position comes 0.1 NM to the NAV-LINE, alarm is indicated by message and
buzzer sounds. This alarming can be used an alert to know when own ship deviated from
established course or entered in the alarming area.

NAV-LINE Alarm

ALARM MESSAGE BUZZER CONDITION

NAV LINE peep peep Own ship comes 0.1 NM close to NAV-LINE
Resettable

w-. GYro Alarm: GYRO SET ......ccccccriimmimcnensnsssssssnsssssssssscsssmsssssssssans

The system’s NSK (North Stabilizer Kit) receives bearing signal from Gyro Compass. Even -
while the system’s power supply is shut off, system follows the Gyro. Only when the Gyro
itself is stopped operation or some abnormalities occur in Gyro signal line, it ceases follow up.
When the Gyro compass is restored normal operation, GYRO SET alarm is indicated but without
alarm buzzer sound. Accordingly, set up of Gyro synchronization is required whenever the Gyro
alarm is indicated.

B 8 TRIAL MANEUVER SIMULATION

TRIAL MANEUVER
COURSE SPEED DELAY TIME

g

DATA ENTRY

ENENEN
4 ]s]le]

o -]
EIRE

The purpose of TRIALL. MANEUVER function is to make a simulation of course, speed and
delay time to avoid possible collision against dangerous target. Range of simulation for course,
speed and delay time are as follows:

Course: Upto 180° for port and starboard (1° step)

Speed: 0 to 50 KTS (0.1 KTS step)

Delay time: 0 to 60 MIN (1 MIN step)
Result of simulation is displayed on the screen by vector with digital indication of CPA and
TCPA.
In Trial Maneuver, simulation is carried out on condition that objective ship’s course and speed
are unchanged as it is. Therefore, MIN CPA and MIN TCPA should be set up with surplus
allowance to off set the difference from actual maneuvering of the own ship.

<. SiMulation by True Vector (TRUE VECTOR) ..covieceeeceecnccnsrnesnnennnas

In True Vector mode, calculation is made on the basis of TRIAL SPEED and TRIAL COURSE
and the result is displayed on the screen in dotted line as the variation of own vector as illus-
trated in next page (own ship turns to starboard).

The sketch shows that the port forward dangerous target changed to safe target in result of trial
simulation. Accompanied numerical data of CPA and TCPA resulted from simulation are indicated
on ARPA data frame on the screen.

Test video

For the target under acquisition or tracking, this is to select the test video to be used for the

will be sufficient to check if the test video of No. 7 “HIT” is to be displayed in normal opera-

Normally, test video is carried out by NO. 7 “HIT”. In this case, the test videos are displayed
0.06 to 0.28 miles outside of the target echo under acquired and tracking as the sketch below.

Targets echo

Test video No. 7 HIT

NOTE

Test video will not be displayed for those targets not acquired nor under tracking and also
when the [GAIN] and [CLUTTER SEA] knobs are not adequately controlled.

When a clear target is initially acquired by manual but the test video of NO.7 HIT is not
displayed and unable to track, there is the possibility of improper setting of quantized level
otherwise PC4417 ARPA is supposed to be faulty.

[l] Quantized Level Must not change this set value unwarrantedly)
Quantization level is to determine the minimum signal level of video input to the target detection
circuit of ARPA.

In this test, quantization level can be set up within the range of 1 to 63.

If this value is more reduced, more weaker signals will input to the target detect circuit. How-
ever, noise from the radar will be also increased to disturb significantly the function of target
acquisition and tracking. It is importdnt to set the value 4 to 5 points larger on top of the value

where noises start to detect.
Procedures to set Quantization to 32 are as follows:
1. Press [TEST] switch.

4. When required to change the value, enter New value and press [ENT] switch.

=B} TRUE AND FALSE ECHOES ON DISPLAY

The role of radar operator is to analyze echoes on the screen and helps maneuvering the vessel
in maximum performance. For that purpose, operator should know very well about advantages
and weak points of the Radar in analyzing echoes on the screen. It is important to get more
experiences of practicing radar observation in good visibility and in comparison with actual lands
and islands in sight.

The substantial usages of radar are watch keeping of own ship and other ships course in open
sea, recognition of bouys or navigation marks when entering port, own ship’s position relative to
lands and islands by measuring distance and bearing on the chart and further application to grasp
position and movement of the clouds of heavy rain. The following section explains the symptoms
appearing on the screen.

....... Radar Wave with the Horlzon

Radar wave has a character to propagate along the curvature of the earth, This character varies
depending upon the conditions of the propagating atmosphere. In a normal propagation mode,
distance of propagation upto the horizontal line is approx. 10% longer than the distance of optical
horizontal line as a formula below:

D = 2.23 (Vil + Vh2) mile

h1: Height of radar scanner above sea level (m)
h2: Height of an object above sea level (m)

In normal propagation, maximum detective range of an object restricted by curvature of the earth
can be obtained from FIG. 6.1.

For example when the height of own ship’s radar scanner is 10m.

a) Required height of an object observable on radar screen at the detective range of 64 miles
is more than 660m.

b) When the height of an object is 10m, detective range is approx. 15 miles.
However, the maximum detective range of the object either prolonged or shortened slightly
depends upon the size of the object or by the weather conditions.

........ Strength of Reflection from the Targets......ccccoomiiicerrinninseenccnn

Strength of reflection from the targets depends not only on its height but also on materials and
shapes that reflection from higher and larger targets are not always stronger. Specially the coastal
lines are influenced by its ups and downs significantly that as FIG. 6.2, care must be taken that
inside highland only appears on the screen but low land coastal lines are not seen on the screen.

The high land appears on the screen

This slope does not appear on the screen

FIG. 6.2

When the sea is rough bright noise reflected from waves extended around the center of screen.
Higher the waves, brighter the noises. Sometimes the turbulence of sea current also caused
reflection and presented on the screen as a fake coastal line.

S - 1 £ T =3 =L 3 Lo 1=

Actually what does not exist but appears on the screen is called the False echoes. Causes of false
echoes are as follows:

[1] Shadow

Owing to the location of scanner, surrounding structure of funnel and mast may block the passage
of radar wave propagation. This shadow can be distinguished in the picture of sea clutters where
the shadow exist, that part of sea clutters are thin or blank. Since shadows are sea clutters are
thin or blank. Since shadows are fixed of its direction and size, it is important to caution that
some targets in this area may be over looked.

--- PAGE 204 ---
[
[ X J
6.1 TRUE AND FALSE ECHOES ON DISPLAY eee

[lI] Side lobe

Radar beam has a main beam and side lobes of a small energy. By the effect of the sidelobe, a
small energy. By the effect of the sidelobe, arc formed series of echoes sometimes appear at the
same distance as true target in FIG. 6.3. Distinction of these false echoes are comparatively easy
when the true target is clear and independent.

FIG. 6.3
[l False Echo by Secondary Reflection
When an object is in the vicinity of own ship and in some occasion, 2 echoes appear on the
screen that one is true echo and another is a false echo resulted from secondary reflection of
radar beam from ship’s structure such as funnel or mast. In this case, false echo appears in the

direction of funnel or mast from the radar scanner as FIG. 6.4.

Direct radar beam passage

True target

Radar scanner

False target due to
Secondary reflection passage secondary reflection

Funnel

FIG. 6.4

[IV] False Echo by Multiple Reflection

As FIG. 6.5, when the own ship is in side by side with a large ship or building, evenly spaced
plural echoes appear in one direction. In this case, true echo is the nearest echo to the own ship.

FIG. 6.5

--- PAGE 205 ---
[Vl

[vil

Abnormal Propagation

Maximum detectable range of the radar can be determined by the height of the radar scanner and
the object as mentioned in section “Radar wave with Horizon”. However, depending upon the
atmospheric condition such as formation of “Duct”, radar wave propagates through the Duct and
illogically faraway target is detected.

For example, when the radar is set on 6 mile range with the pulse repetition of 1000 Hz, and if
the first pulse returned from the target more than 81 miles far were received at the timing of next
pulse repetition the false image is presented on a position about 81 miles deducted from the
actual distance.

Should a false image appear in a distance of 5 miles on the screen, this target’s true distance is
5 + 81 = 85 miles. When the radar is set on 1.5 mile range with 2000 Hz pulse repetition, the
false echo may appear on a position about 40 miles deducted from the actual distance.

This kind of false echo can be discriminated by changing range scale (changing pulse repetition
frequency) so as to see the distance of echo changes.

Radar Interference

When the other ship’s radar operated in same frequency is in the vicinity of own ship, Radar
interference pattern appears on the screen as the FIG. 6.6. As interference pattern appears in a
form of dotted stripes and moves around the screen but the pattern is not stationary, discrimination
from the true target is rather easy.

— L ROUTINE MAINTENANCE

In order to maintain the radar always in normal condition, following maintenance works are
indispensable. It is recommentable to apply a regular maintenance work on each part or unit to
minimize the possible failures.

Point of general maintenance works commonly applicable to each until is as follows:

1))

2)

3)

4)

Keep the equipment clean

Clean the dust, stain and salt sticked on the casing by dry linen cloth. Air filters or air
ventilation openings have to be cleaned of its dust by brushing to make better air flow.
*Turn off the power supply before carry out maintenance jobs.

Tightness of Bolts and Nuts
Check regularly the tightness of bolts and nuts fixing the frames, units and components.

Printed Circuit Board
Check corrosion, rust spots and condition of soldering points! Clean dirt, dry salt or frost on
PCB. Oxidized spots on wire strap have to be cleaned off before causing circuit disconnection.

Cabling

Check condition of cable connection between modules, PCB, and units. Specially tightness
or fitting condition of connectors and terminal boards. Those sockets fitted on CRT and
Magnetrons have to be checked of its fitting states and electrial conductivity.

--- PAGE 208 ---
—7_ 2 MAINTENANCE ON EACH UNIT

e SCaNNer NKE-3500-2 (JMA-8313-CA, JMA-8613-CA) ...................

Maintenance on scanner has to be carried out after confirming the power supply switch and
scanner safety switch is in “OFF” position. Must not forget to return scanner safety switch back
to “ON” position after job on the scanner is finished.

19)

2)

M

2

3

Radiator

Front of the scanner radiator is normally exposed to the soot, salt, dust or the paint splash.
These stains cause attenuation and reflection to the normal radiation of the radar wave and
worsen the radar performance. Cleaning of radiator front must be carried out by using a
linen cloth soaked with fresh water or alcohol.

Caution
Never use such solvent as Gasoline, Benzene, Trichloroethylene or Kentene for cleaning.

Scanner Mechanism

Supply oil seal

Every 6 months, about 100 grams of grease (Mobilux 2) must be recharged in oil seal
chamber through the grease nipple by the grease gun. The suitable amount of supplying
grease is to fill up the grease chamber and a part of grease is start to overflow. (Mobile
products: MOBILUX-2)

Oiling Gear

For the main shaft driving gear and encoder driving gear, grease MOBILUX-2 must be
spread evenly on gear teeth by a spreader or by brush. Recommendable frequency of oiling
is every 6 months or at least every year. However, the more frequently oiling applied, the
more longer the life of the gear can be prolonged.

Replacement of grease in the grease pot for the main shaft gear must be done every 2 years.
(Mobile products: MOBILUX-2)

Oiling Reduction Gear

Grease for the Reduction Gear should be Esso standard products BEACON 325 or Maruzen
products WR-No.2. Never use other grease than above unless otherwise proved of its
homogeneity.

®  Supplying oil
Grease to be supplied by grease gun from grease nipple in the lower part of reduction
gear for once every 6 months. Suitable amount of a supplying grease is about 80 to 100
grams. Excess supply of grease than above amount may cause stiring heat or worsen
the efficiency of lubrication.

® Replacement of Oil
Interval of grease replacement depends on the condition of radar usage. However,
recommendable interval is every 27000 hours running or 5 to 6 years which ever is
earlier. In replacing grease, disassemble the reduction gear, remove old grease and fill
up with new grease.

--- PAGE 209 ---
(4) Scanner Pedestal

3)

Pedestal with fixing bolts must be checked regularly of its condition of corrosion or sticking
due to rust. Recommendable time of painting will be every 6 months.

Adjustment of Wide Angle V-Belt

Wide angle V-belt is used for linkage between drive motor and reduction gear. Groove
angle of this belt being 60 degrees, grip against pulley is firm and steady compare to 30
degrees V-belts due to less sinkage that slacking of tension occurs in quite few cases.
Adjustment of tension is thus rarely required but proper tension should be maintained. For
this purpose, regular check of V-belt to be carried out (at least every 6 months) and if
slacking of belt being found, following adjustment to be done.

Put a weight of approx 2.0 + 0.3 kg on the center part of V-belt hopped around driven
motor and reduction gear and at the tension of about 3.3 mm sinkage, adjust the drive
motor’s fixing position by fastening 4 bolts. Care must be taken that for the wide angle V-
belt having smaller cross section area, feeling of tension is more higher than other V-belt
under same tension.

When replaced with the new V-belt, put weight load of 2.3 kg + 0.3 kg on the new V-belt
and at the tension of abt. 3.3 mm sinkage, further turn the drive motor for 10 to 15 minutes
and again place a weight of 2.0 + 0.3 kg and at the sinkage of 3.3 mm, fix the position of
drive motor by fastening 4 fixing bolts. Until accustomed to get the value of applying weight
and sinkage, it will be better to use weighing scale or tension meter. Adjustment of drive
motor’s position must be done by adjusting 4 fixing bolts while rocking motor clamping
plate by lever action of screw driver. After the adjustment, be sure to fasten 4 motor fixing
bolts firmly.

Scanner Mechanism

Supply oil seal
Every 6 months, about 20 grams of grease (Mobilux 2) must be recharged in oil seal chamber
through the grease nipple by the grease gun. The suitable amount of supplying grease is to
fill up the grease chamber and a part of grease is start to overflow. (Mobile products:
MOBILUX-2)

Oiling Gear

For the main shaft driving gear and encoder driving gear, grease MOBILUX-2 must be
spread evenly on gear teeth by a spreader or by brush. Recommendable frequency of oiling
is at least every 6 months. However, the more frequently oiling applied, the more longer the
life of the gear can be prolonged. (Mobile products: MOBILUX-2)

Drive Motor

Condition of commutator and brush must be checked every 200 hours run and whenever
these are remarkably wom out, replacement must be done.

(Brush: standard spare). Standard life of the brush are about 3000 hours. However, length of
the brush became 2/3 of the full length (where the worn out notch marked) is the suitable
chance for renewal. Commutator should always e kept clean and if the surface is stained
with persistent dirt with carbon dust, fine sand paper (No.150 ~ 400) may be used for clean

up.

Scanner Pedestal
Pedestal with fixing bolts must be checked regularly of its condition of corrosion or sticking
due to rust. Recommendable time of painting will be every 6 months.

@ Supplying oil
Grease to be supplied by grease gun from grease nipple in the lower part of reduction

Excess supply of grease than above amount way cause stiring heat or worsen the
efficiency of lubrication.

® Replacement of Qil
Interval of grease replacement depends on the condition of radar usage. However,
recommendable interval is every 20000 hours running or 3 to 4 years which every is
earlier. In replacing grease, disassemble the reduction gear, remove old grease and fill
up with new grease.

Drive motor and Reduction gear are linked by V-belt. The V-belt may slack in a long
running of the radar and adjustment of tension is required in due course.

Put the load of approx. 0.6 kg on the center part of V-belt hooped around drive motor and
reduction gear and at the tension of about 2.5 mm bending down, adjust the drive motor’s
fixing position by fastening 4 bolts.

When replaced with a new V-belt, applying load will be 0.7 kg and bending down distance
will be approx. 2.5 mm where drive motor’s fixing position must be determined by fastening
4 bolts. To get the correct value of load (weight), use weighing scale or tension meter if
available. Adjustment of drive motor’s position must be done by fastening 4 fixing bolts
while rocking motor housing by hand until proper position is determined. After the adjustment,
be sure to fasten firmly the 4 fixing bolts.

Scanner Pedestal

Pedestal with fixing bolts must be checked regularly of its condition of corrosion or sticking
due to rust. Painting is the best solution against corrosion so that recommendable interval of
painting will be every 6 months.

Oiling and Replacement of Grease in Reduction Gear

once every 2 years and suitable amount of a supplying grease is about 30 to 40 grams
per once. Excess supply of grease than above amount may cause stiring heat or worsen

recommendable interval is every 27,000 hours running or 5 to 6 years which ever is

Adjustment of Wide Angle V-belt

Wide angle V-belt is used for linkage between drive motor and reduction gear. Groove
angle of this belt being 60 degrees, grip against pulley is firm and steady compare to 30
degrees type V-belt due to less sinkage that slacking of tension occurs in quite few cases.
Adjustment of tension is thus rarely required but proper tension should be maintained. For
this purpose, regular check of V-belt to be carried out at least every 6 months and if slacking
of belt being found, following adjustment to be done.

Put a load of approx. 1.5 £ 0.3 kg on the center part of V-belt hoped around drive motor
and reduction gear and at the tension of about 2.5 mm sinkage, adjust the drive motor’s
fixing position by fastening 4 bolts. Care must be taken that for wide angle V-belt having
smaller cross section area feeling of tension is more higher than other V-belt under same
tension. When replaced with the new V-belt, put a load of 1.8 £ 0.3 kg on the V-belt and at
the tension of abt. 2.5 mm sinkage, further turn the drive motor for 10 to 15 minutes and
again place a load of 1.5 + 0.3 kg and at the sinkage of 2.5 mm fix the position of drive
motor by fastening 4 fixing bolts. Until accustomed to get the value of applying weight and
sinkage, it will be better to use weighing scale or tension meter.

Adjustment of drive motor’s position may well be done by lever action by using screw
driver against motor clamping plate with loosening and fastening 4 fixing bolts.

After the adjustment, be sure to fasten firmly the 4 fixing bolts.

Cleaning of CRT Surface

Accumulated dust on the CRT surface deteriorate the transparency of the screen and worsen
the image resolution. Cleaning of CRT surface must be done by wet linen cloth. Never use
such solvent as gasoline or thinners and never scrub CRT surface by dry cloth.

Cleaning of High Voltage Compartment

Remove the dust around CRT anode cap and high voltage lead wire by dry linen cloth or by
feather brush.

Excess accumulation of dust may deteriorate insulation against high voltage.

Replacement of Backup Battery
When the life of memory backup battery come to an end, system alarm sounds. Ask for the
new for details)

........ Coaxial Cable (JMA-8313-CA, JMA-8613-CA)......ccoceremmmmmmererrrrnns

(1

)

3

4

Coaxial pipe gland at the end of coaxial cable is applied with water tight treatment at the
time of new installation. However, regular check is required to prevent water leakage through
the damage at the gland part. It is recommendable to apply painting on coaxial pipe gland
every 6 months.

Do not inflict heavy weight or impact on the coaxial cable.

Coaxial cable’s limit of elastic deformation is about 250 kg. However against impact load,
elasticity is far fragile. Great care must be taken to keep coaxial cable away from the
mechanical damage on board.

Do not inflict twist and extension force on coaxial cable.

For further details see “Installation manual for S band coaxial cable”.

Wave Guide (JMA-8263-7CA/9CA, IMA-8513-7CA/9CA)

Wave guide being exposed to weather, there is the possibility of water leakage through
loose junction point due to vibration or corrosion by oxidization. Periodic check of each
flange section and painting of wave guide are indispensable.

Care must be taken to keep wave guide away from the mechanical damage on board.

Br] General

This radar is designed to withstand possible severe ambient conditions by using semiconductors
(except for few special tubes) to upgrade the reliability of the circuits so that otherwise occurrence
of external adverse cause or human error, equipment is almost free from the troubles. That
equipment’s normal condition can be maintained by applying periodic function check and
maintenance mentioned hereunder.

--- PAGE 216 ---
—8 2 FUNCTION CHECK

Check should be carried out regularly and whenever abnormalities being found, investigate the
cause of trouble. In checking, care must be taken specially for high voltage circuits. Careless
check or miss-judgement may cause adverse consequences. It is more effective to make it a rule
to record the results of check for comparison with the next time of checking.

indicates error message list (2).

—8-3 TROUBLE SHOOTING

Semi-conductors are highly reliable components that except in a few cases of defects due to
under estimated design and factory check or due to the human error, failure of semi-conductors
are not so often exist. However, comparatively grater part of the cause of the trouble are such as
due to high value resistor’s damage by humidity, variable resistors wear down or poor contacts
of switches and relays.

Apart from the failure of components, there are de-tuning of circuit due to improper adjustment
or loosed connection of cables due to lack of maintenance. It is more effective to make a check
and adjustment taking these points into consideration.

In case of the Fuse burnt out, check should be carried out to trace the back ground of over
current even after the replacement. However it should be noted that the each fuse has its own
varied cut off time.

Fuse list is provided in Table. 8.4.

The system has a [TEST] switch with which function check can be carried out by a simple
operation.

j%: Countermeasures to Trouble

Electric circuits of this radar is so complicated that in the event of trouble, it is recommendable
to ask for the engineer’s attendance otherwith ask for the technical advise/instruction for the
countermeasures to the troubles.

There are the possibility of occurrence of such a simple cause of trouble as mentioned below.

(1)  Loosed connection of cables at the terminals on each units

a) Loosed connection of cable rag and terminal

b) Poor workmanship of cable end process resulted earthing or short circuit to the adjoining
circuit

c) Disconnection of cable due to vibration

(2)  Loosed contact of connector pins and sockets in the equipment

Note
The system has a standard spare parts as the list Table 8.5.

—8- B REPLACEMENT OF MAJOR PARTS

Replacement of parts should be carried out after the main power supply turned off.

Note
Magnetron is located in Modulator unit that due to the influence of powerful magnetic flux, it is
recommendable to keep the wrist watch away from the modulator compartment.

....-... Replacement of (V201) Magnetron ..........ccccvrcromimmincinnssnmnnasnnnncns
(JMA-8313-CA, JMA-8613-CA)

Remove sealed cover on modulator unit and check that there are no residue high voltage in
modulator high voltage circuit. Remove socket of magnetron then remove 4 magnetron fixing
bolts so that magnetron can be took off. In replacing magnetron, keep any magnetic material
such as screw drivers or radio tools away from the new magnetron. After the replacement, lead
wire must be correctly connected.

NOTE

1) To discharge the residue high voltage, hold insulated grip of a screw driver while one
end of metallic part earthed on the ground, bring another end to touch L201, anode of
CD2106 (casing side), both end of R2201/R2202, to release the residue high voltage.
(In case of transceiver NTG-3060, discharging points are cathode of CD-2202/CD2205,
and both end of R2203/R2206. For NTG-3050, cathode of CD2310/CD2311 and both
end of R2305/R2310.)
Special attention must be paid for dangerous high voltage.

2) Remarks for the handling of magnetron stored in a long time
Long stored magnetron is required following aging process, or otherwise the magnetron
will be damaged by sparking or short life.

(1) Take longer pre-heating time. (maintain 20 to 30 minutes ST-BY condition)

(2) Start operation from short pulse range and gradually switch to long pulse operation.
If the function becomes unstable, turn the radar back to ST-BY condition for further
5 to 10 minutes’ aging and resume operation.

(3) Tuning adjustment must be carried out after abt. 15 minutes transmission.

(4) Adjust RV30 in receiver unit to get tuning indication of 4 or 5 points in TUNE
indicator on display unit.

(5) Tuning adjustment for the radar without interswitch unit (NQE-889/723) can be
referred to the description in “Tuning Adjustment* in 8.6 ADJUSTMENT.

(6) tuning adjustment for the radar connected with interswitch unit (NQE-889/723)
can be referred to ”Adjustment in case of radar connected with interswitch unit in
8.6 ADJUSTMENT.

fitting direction that arrow marked direction meets with the front end side.

..... Replacement of (A302) PIN AHENUALOT ......ccuceeeresmrerersesresmensesesens
(JMA-8263-6CA-9CA, JMA-85130-7CA/9CA)

Remove PIN attenuator after removing 8 fixing bolts. Care must be taken for the thickness of the
spacer sandwiching the PIN attenuator. The spacer to be used between PIN attenuator and front
end is 10 mm in thickness. '

NOE—n— —-————————
Above work is only applicable for JMA-8262-6CA/9CA, IMA-8263-7CA/9CA, IMA-8513-
TCA./9CA, type radars.

........ Replacement of (V1) (o3 3 (P

(CRT having weight of abt. 30 kg, work requires 2 men or more).

Remove the bolts below operation panel first. Then take off front frame. Take off 4 bolts which
fixing the CRT on the casing. Lift CRT carefully until CRT screen comes up to horizontal level.
On this stage, CRT is self supported by spring. Remove the wiring coming to the CRT.

Then one man holds the CRT to prevent from falling down and another man removes the 2
screws clamping the CRT supporting spring out of casing (right and left 2). Further remove the
stopper attachment in hinged part. 2 men in cooperation lift off the CRT out of casing and place
the CRT on safe and soft place face down.

Disassemble socket, deflection coil, anode terminal and CRT clamping frame out of CRT by
removing 4 M8 screws.

In fitting new CRT, reverse procedure is applicable.

CAUTION
CRT being fragile glass product, special care must be taken in carrying out replacement
work not to touch any hard tools or materials nor to place CRT on any hard materials.

—8 B ADJUSTMENTS

........ Adjustment of NSK unit suitable to Gyro compass ........ccceeeee-.

This radar’s NSK unit is of a solid states and is compatible to most of the gyrocompasses of
various manufacturers only by switch setting (for step motor type: DC35V-DC90, for synchro
motor type: primary exciter voltage of AC50V-AC110V).

Before starting up, be sure to set up the Gyro selector switch S1,52 and S3 in NSK circuit
(PC4201) in accordance with the tables provided here under. Normally, at the time of shipment
from the factory, Gyro selector switches are set up in synchromotor type of turning ratio 360x. In
actual setting up, check the type of ship’s gyrocompass and follow the instruction as described in
the table 8.6.1, 8.6.2, and 8.6.3.

(1) Setting S2

There are 2 kinds of Gyrocompass signal output that one is STEP and another is SYNCHRO.
Check ship’s Gyro carefully and set S2 in accordance with the following table.

Table 8.6.1 Setting table for Gyro select switch (S2)

<Substantial adjustment has been finished in the factory. However, following should be carried

--- PAGE 231 ---
.eeenne BEAING AdJustment ... e

Bearing adjustment can be done by turning switches S1, S2 and S3 on PC4414 in the Display
unit.

(1) Operate [BEARING] switch in operation panel and make radar in (HEAD UP) mode.

(2) By using ship’s Gyrocompass, catch a proper target (example: anchoring ship, breakwater or
a bouy) and measure the bearing from own ship’s heading line (example: 25°).

(3) Pick up above target on the radar screen and measure the bearing by EBL. If the result has
an error, correction must be made by following procedures.

(4) Put EBL on the bearing measured in above (2) (e.x. 25°).

(5) By using a escrew driver, adjust [bearing adj] switches S1, S2 and S3 on PC4414 so that
subjected target aligned on EBL.
A step increment of [BEARING ADJ] switch S1, is abt. 22.5°, 82, is abt. 1.4° and S3 is abt.
0.09°. Depending on the value of an error, adjust each switch in combination to correct
bearing.

... True Bearing Adjustment ... iiiiiciiciiriecs e

Press [INITIAL] switch on operation panel. Message ” (1)' gyx;o sgT . displayed on left side of
screen.

Enter [1] by tenkey. Then input the bearing value wanted to setup. This bearing value indicated
lower right side of the screen. Press [ENT] switch so that initial setting of Gyro data is programmed
and present Gyro data is indicated upper right corner of the screen.

... RAaNge Adjustment (PC4411) .......cererecerecer s s e smesnnmneens

The purpose of this adjustment is to make the correct indication of target’s distance on the
display screen.

(1) Pick up a target of actual distance that is known on the chart and measure its distance on
the radar screen.
(Preferable to select a target in short range. Because if select a target in long range, an error
becomes larger in short range).

(2) 1If there is an error between actual distance and the distance measured on the radar screen,
adjust [A-O MILE] RV2 on PC4411 in display unit by a &crew driver so that both distances
become the same.

e TUNING Adjustment (PC4411) ... e s eenene s

(1) Set [TUNE] knob in center position.

(2) Turn [A-TUNE] (RV7) on PC4411 so that echoes on the screen become most clear.
(Condition: A radar in transmitting states)

.--.... Adjustment of Performance Monitor (NJU-13/14/15) PC6200

(1

® OO

Preparation

Set range to 38 NM and make radar in transmitting condition.

Adjust [TUNE] knob to get maximum tuning point.

Tum [GAIN] knob fully clockwith and tune [ANTI-CLUTTER SEA] knob fully counter
clockwise.

Tum [PERF MON] switch “ON” position.

Check if “Bar-meter” of the performance monitor is extended to approx. in center. (Bar-
meter length can be controlled by [=] key on the control panel) Adjust RV5 (PM
TUNE) so that brilliancy of performance monitor becomes maximum.

Stop the turning of scanner by switch S5002 (SCANNER) in rectifier unit of display unit
(In case of 2 unit type, this switch is S5502 in rectifier unit for 2 unit type).

Leave the radar in transmitting condition.

Face the scanner radiator against the sensor front of the performance monitor.

NOTE
Facing angle of scanner against performance monitor to be 4 degrees delayed since
scanner’s main beam pattern is 4 degrees advanced.

ELIMINATING THE INTERFERENCE ON FREQUENCIES USED FOR MARINE
COMMUNICATIONS AND NAVIGATION DUE TO OPERATION OF THE RADAR.
ALL CABLES OF THE RADAR ARE TO BE RUN AWAY FROM THE CABLES
OF RADIO EQUIPMENT (EX. RADIOTELEPHONE, COMMUNICATIONS RECEIVER
AND DIRECTION FINDER ETC.). ESPECIALLY INTER—WIRING CABLES
BETWEEN SCANNER UNIT AND DIiSPLAY UNIT OF THE RADAR SHOULD

SETTING OF SPEED LOG SWITCH (REFER TO Fig.141)

1. There are 6 kinds of speed Logs which are considered as “standards® for this radar.

Pulse Types: 400 pulses/nm, 200 pulses/nm. 100 pulses/nm
Synchro Types: 360X/nm., 180X/nm. 90X/nm.
Simultaneous input of 2 systems of above type can be made and selection of either
system can be made by switch S16 @
2. Input of speed log signals are connected to TB420! . however. the pulse types are
connected to side and synchro types are connected to side. Each
sides have input terminals of 2 series A and B. side and [=] side. the [=] side shall
be connected to ground in the case of using pulse type. so please be careful.
3. S11 14 is pulse type/synchro type seletor switch.

Set to ON whichever you are going to use. pulse type or synchro type. from either
“A" log or "B° log.
